\doxysubsubsection*{:\+ A string which is used in /\+proc/\+slabinfo to identify this cache.}
kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create\+\_\+usercopy -\/ Create a kmem cache with a region suitable for copying to userspace.

@size:\+ The size of objects to be created in this cache. @align:\+ The required alignment for the objects. @flags:\+ SLAB flags @useroffset:\+ Usercopy region offset @usersize:\+ Usercopy region size @ctor:\+ A constructor for the objects, or NULL.

This is a legacy wrapper, new code should use either \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a277399776a697be7c5cba7aa9143019e}{KMEM\+\_\+\+CACHE\+\_\+\+USERCOPY()} if whitelisting a single field is sufficient, or \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_adf8cdc761f51d67846ff22c9b5798a58}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create()} with the necessary parameters passed via the args parameter (see \&struct \doxylink{structkmem__cache__args}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+args})

Return:\+ a pointer to the cache on success, NULL on failure.

Function Description:\+ Creates a kmem cache with a usercopy region specified. This is a legacy wrapper for creating caches that have a region safe for copying to/\+from userspace. New code should use \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a277399776a697be7c5cba7aa9143019e}{KMEM\+\_\+\+CACHE\+\_\+\+USERCOPY()} instead. \begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

\+\_\+\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+flex -\/ Allocate an object that has a trailing flexible array @\+KMALLOC:\+ kmalloc wrapper function to use for allocation. @\+GFP:\+ GFP flags for the allocation. @\+TYPE:\+ type of structure to allocate space for. @\+FAM:\+ The name of the flexible array member of @\+TYPE structure. @\+COUNT:\+ how many @\+FAM elements to allocate space for.

Returns:\+ Newly allocated pointer to @\+TYPE with @\+COUNT-\/many trailing @\+FAM elements, or NULL on failure or if @\+COUNT cannot be represented by the member of @\+TYPE that counts the @\+FAM elements (annotated via \+\_\+\+\_\+counted\+\_\+by()).

kmalloc\+\_\+track\+\_\+caller is a special version of kmalloc that records the calling function of the routine calling it for slab leak tracking instead of just the calling function (confusing, eh?). It\textquotesingle{}s useful when the call to kmalloc comes from a widely-\/used standard allocator where we care about the real place the memory allocation request comes from.

\end{DoxyCode}
Please use this macro to create slab caches. Simply specify the name of the structure and maybe some flags that are listed above.

The alignment of the struct determines object alignment. If you f.\+e. add \+\_\+\+\_\+\+\_\+\+\_\+cacheline\+\_\+aligned\+\_\+in\+\_\+smp to the struct declaration then the objects will be properly aligned in SMP configurations.

Macro Description:\+ Creates a kmem cache for a structure type. It takes the structure name and flags, and automatically sets the alignment to the structure\textquotesingle{}s alignment. This is the recommended way to create caches for structures.

kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create -\/ Create a kmem cache. \+\_\+\+\_\+name:\+ A string which is used in /\+proc/\+slabinfo to identify this cache. \+\_\+\+\_\+object\+\_\+size:\+ The size of objects to be created in this cache. \+\_\+\+\_\+args:\+ Optional arguments, see \&struct \doxylink{structkmem__cache__args}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+args}. Passing NULL means defaults will be used for all the arguments.

This is currently implemented as a macro using {\ttfamily \+\_\+\+Generic()} to call either the new variant of the function, or a legacy one.

The new variant has 4 parameters:\+ {\ttfamily \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_adf8cdc761f51d67846ff22c9b5798a58}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create(name, object\+\_\+size, args, flags)}}

See \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a01ef3c3389f42bf1de2af0cc515e0e51}{\+\_\+\+\_\+kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create\+\_\+args()} which implements this.

The legacy variant has 5 parameters:\+ {\ttfamily \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_adf8cdc761f51d67846ff22c9b5798a58}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create(name, object\+\_\+size, align, flags, ctor)}}

The align and ctor parameters map to the respective fields of \&struct \doxylink{structkmem__cache__args}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+args}

Context:\+ Cannot be called within a interrupt, but can be interrupted.

Return:\+ a pointer to the cache on success, NULL on failure.

Function Description:\+ This is the main macro for creating a kmem cache. It uses \+\_\+\+Generic() to handle both the new 4-\/parameter variant and the legacy 5-\/parameter variant. This provides a unified interface for cache creation.

To whitelist a single field for copying to/\+from usercopy, use this macro instead for \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a6084b6febfcc20d1267bd92bff862f09}{KMEM\+\_\+\+CACHE()} above.

Macro Description:\+ Creates a kmem cache with a whitelisted usercopy field. It takes the structure name, flags, and field name to whitelist. This is used for caches that need to copy data to/\+from userspace.

define SLAB\+\_\+\+HWCACHE\+\_\+\+ALIGN -\/ Align objects on cache line boundaries.

Sufficiently large objects are aligned on cache line boundary. For object size smaller than a half of cache line size, the alignment is on the half of cache line size. In general, if object size is smaller than 1/\+2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}n of cache line size, the alignment is adjusted to 1/\+2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}n.

If explicit alignment is also requested by the respective \&struct \doxylink{structkmem__cache__args}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+args} field, the greater of both is alignments is applied.

Prevent merging with compatible kmem caches. This flag should be used cautiously. Valid use cases:\+


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item caches created for self-\/tests (e.\+g. kunit)
\item general caches created and used by a subsystem, only when a (subsystem-\/specific) debug option is enabled
\item performance critical caches, should be very rare and consulted with slab maintainers, and not used together with CONFIG\+\_\+\+SLUB\+\_\+\+TINY 
\end{DoxyItemize}

{\bfseries Value:\+}
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{(\doxymbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a6d47fa2014fd28cb10b103fe892e030c}{KMALLOC\_MIN\_SIZE}}\ <\ 16\ ?\ \(\backslash\)}
\DoxyCodeLine{(\doxymbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a6d47fa2014fd28cb10b103fe892e030c}{KMALLOC\_MIN\_SIZE}})\ :\ 16)}

\end{DoxyCode}
This restriction comes from byte sized index implementation. Page size is normally 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}12 bytes and, in this case, if we want to use byte sized index which can represent 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}8 entries, the size of the object should be equal or greater to 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}12 /\+ 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}8 = 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}4 = 16. If minimum size of kmalloc is less than 16, we use it as minimum object size and give up to use byte sized index. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_source_l00649}{649}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_source}{slab.\+h}}.

\Hypertarget{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a76aff4e693dc29c222939b24b2e6b9e6}\index{slab.h@{slab.h}!SLAB\_TYPESAFE\_BY\_RCU@{SLAB\_TYPESAFE\_BY\_RCU}}
\index{SLAB\_TYPESAFE\_BY\_RCU@{SLAB\_TYPESAFE\_BY\_RCU}!slab.h@{slab.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{SLAB\_TYPESAFE\_BY\_RCU}{SLAB\_TYPESAFE\_BY\_RCU}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a76aff4e693dc29c222939b24b2e6b9e6} 
\+\#define SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU~\doxymbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_aa88a8cdf415cd49a88af75d817f9d072}{\+\_\+\+\_\+\+SLAB\+\_\+\+FLAG\+\_\+\+BIT}}(\doxymbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a847d50d1b022f898afc8abb6f0fcdf16a60ff282cc2d4ae6199abf83626bf8145}{\+\_\+\+SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU}})}

define SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU -\/ {\bfseries{WARNING}} READ THIS!

This delays freeing the SLAB page by a grace period, it does {\itshape NOT} delay object freeing. This means that if you do \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a3515b5b594edddb8f4910f5a76b9f4bd}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+free()} that memory location is free to be reused at any time. Thus it may be possible to see another object there in the same RCU grace period.

This feature only ensures the memory location backing the object stays valid, the trick to using this is relying on an independent object validation pass. Something like:\+

::\+

begin:\+ rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock(); obj = lockless\+\_\+lookup(key); if (obj) \{ if (!try\+\_\+get\+\_\+ref(obj)) /\+/\+ might fail for free objects rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+unlock(); goto begin;

if (obj-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}key != key) \{ /\+/\+ not the object we expected put\+\_\+ref(obj); rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+unlock(); goto begin; \} \} rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+unlock();

This is useful if we need to approach a kernel structure obliquely, from its address obtained without the usual locking. We can lock the structure to stabilize it and check it\textquotesingle{}s still at the given address, only if we can be sure that the memory has not been meanwhile reused for some other kind of object (which our subsystem\textquotesingle{}s lock might corrupt).

rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock before reading the address, then rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+unlock after taking the spinlock within the structure expected at that address.

Note that object identity check has to be done {\itshape after} acquiring a reference, therefore user has to ensure proper ordering for loads. Similarly, when initializing objects allocated with SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU, the newly allocated object has to be fully initialized {\itshape before} its refcount gets initialized and proper ordering for stores is required. refcount\+\_\+\{add\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}inc\}\+\_\+not\+\_\+zero\+\_\+acquire() and refcount\+\_\+set\+\_\+release() are designed with the proper fences required for reference counting objects allocated with SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU.

Note that it is not possible to acquire a lock within a structure allocated with SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU without first acquiring a reference as described above. The reason is that SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU pages are not zeroed before being given to the slab, which means that any locks must be initialized after each and every kmem\+\_\+struct\+\_\+alloc(). Alternatively, make the ctor passed to \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_adf8cdc761f51d67846ff22c9b5798a58}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create()} initialize the locks at page-\/allocation time, as is done in \+\_\+\+\_\+i915\+\_\+request\+\_\+ctor(), sighand\+\_\+ctor(), and anon\+\_\+vma\+\_\+ctor(). Such a ctor permits readers to safely acquire those ctor-\/initialized locks under rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock() protection.

Note that SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU was originally named SLAB\+\_\+\+DESTROY\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU.

\+\#define ZERO\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+PTR~((void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*})16)}

ZERO\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+PTR will be returned for zero sized kmalloc requests.

Dereferencing ZERO\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+PTR will lead to a distinct access fault.

ZERO\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+PTR can be passed to kfree though in the same way that NULL can. Both make kfree a no-\/op.

\Hypertarget{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a244bededdcadb039793dfa743f79b9d4}\index{slab.h@{slab.h}!kmalloc\_cache\_type@{kmalloc\_cache\_type}}
\index{kmalloc\_cache\_type@{kmalloc\_cache\_type}!slab.h@{slab.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmalloc\_cache\_type}{kmalloc\_cache\_type}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a244bededdcadb039793dfa743f79b9d4} 
enum \doxymbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a244bededdcadb039793dfa743f79b9d4}{kmalloc\+\_\+cache\+\_\+type}}}

Whenever changing this, take care of that \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a42ea7ef5a256dcec25943a0b63384c15}{kmalloc\+\_\+type()} and \doxylink{mm_2slab_8h_a41b4723095f95e821315c3b690d6e44f}{create\+\_\+kmalloc\+\_\+caches()} still work as intended.

KMALLOC\+\_\+\+NORMAL can contain only unaccounted objects whereas KMALLOC\+\_\+\+CGROUP is for accounted but unreclaimable and non-\/dma objects. All the other kmem caches can have both accounted and unaccounted objects.

Enumeration Description:\+ This enumeration defines the different types of kmalloc caches. Types include normal, reclaimable, DMA, and cgroup-\/accounted caches. This allows different allocation types to be served from different cache arrays based on GFP flags. \begin{DoxyEnumFields}[2]{Enumerator}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\_\_alloc\_size()}{\_\_alloc\_size()}\hspace{0.1cm}{\footnotesize\ttfamily [1/2]}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a7e9fa94904622bbae9915cfbedfe5169} 
\+\_\+\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+size (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{1}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

kmalloc -\/ allocate kernel memory @size:\+ how many bytes of memory are required. @flags:\+ describe the allocation context

kmalloc is the normal method of allocating memory for objects smaller than page size in the kernel.

The allocated object address is aligned to at least ARCH\+\_\+\+KMALLOC\+\_\+\+MINALIGN bytes. For @size of power of two bytes, the alignment is also guaranteed to be at least to the size. For other sizes, the alignment is guaranteed to be at least the largest power-\/of-\/two divisor of @size.

The @flags argument may be one of the GFP flags defined at \doxylink{gfp__types_8h}{include/\+linux/\+gfp\+\_\+types.\+h} and described at :\+ref:\+{\ttfamily Documentation/\+core-\/api/\+mm-\/api.\+rst \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}mm-\/api-\/gfp-\/flags\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}}

The recommended usage of the @flags is described at :\+ref:\+{\ttfamily Documentation/\+core-\/api/\+memory-\/allocation.\+rst \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}memory\+\_\+allocation\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}}

Below is a brief outline of the most useful GFP flags

GFP\+\_\+\+KERNEL Allocate normal kernel ram. May sleep.

GFP\+\_\+\+NOWAIT Allocation will not sleep.

GFP\+\_\+\+ATOMIC Allocation will not sleep. May use emergency pools.

Also it is possible to set different flags by OR\textquotesingle{}ing in one or more of the following additional @flags:\+

\+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO Zero the allocated memory before returning. Also see \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a1b52e6826b646b0af0168c19c220859f}{kzalloc()}.

\+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+HIGH This allocation has high priority and may use emergency pools.

\+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOFAIL Indicate that this allocation is in no way allowed to fail (think twice before using).

\+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NORETRY If memory is not immediately available, then give up at once.

\+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOWARN If allocation fails, don\textquotesingle{}t issue any warnings.

\+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+RETRY\+\_\+\+MAYFAIL Try really hard to succeed the allocation but fail eventually.

Function Description:\+ The main kmalloc implementation without allocation profiling. It handles both constant and non-\/constant sizes, selecting the appropriate cache or page allocator. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}.

Function Description:\+ The main kmalloc implementation with node affinity without allocation profiling. It allocates memory from a specific NUMA node. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a54e25ef96b7c67f1ac00e8e9d5bca0d7}{kmalloc\+\_\+node()}.

kzalloc -\/ allocate memory. The memory is set to zero. @size:\+ how many bytes of memory are required. @flags:\+ the type of memory to allocate (see kmalloc).

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed memory without allocation profiling. It calls \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_acac90ecc8811448ce96b30ba6123a4bf}{kmalloc\+\_\+noprof()} with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO added. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a1b52e6826b646b0af0168c19c220859f}{kzalloc()}. Here is the call graph for this function:\+

kmalloc\+\_\+array -\/ allocate memory for an array. ~\newline
:\+ number of elements. @size:\+ element size. @flags:\+ the type of memory to allocate (see kmalloc).

Function Description:\+ Allocates memory for an array of elements without allocation profiling. It multiplies n \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} size with overflow checking. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_af528e4c342be440189fe390a13792cf3}{kmalloc\+\_\+array()}.

Function Description:\+ Allocates memory for an array on a specific NUMA node without allocation profiling. It checks for overflow and calls the node-\/aware kmalloc. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_afe0b9a795b7d2922340cb0de1987ce2e}{kmalloc\+\_\+array\+\_\+node()}.

Function Description:\+ Allocates an array of elements using kvmalloc on a specific node without allocation profiling. It multiplies n \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} size with overflow checking and calls kvmalloc\+\_\+node. \Hypertarget{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a20e04b0db3c7d43dbcf58a62699e1792}\index{slab.h@{slab.h}!\_\_kmalloc\_cache\_node\_noprof@{\_\_kmalloc\_cache\_node\_noprof}}

\+\_\+\+\_\+always\+\_\+inline unsigned int \+\_\+\+\_\+kmalloc\+\_\+index (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{bool}]{size\+\_\+is\+\_\+constant}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

Figure out which kmalloc slab an allocation of a certain size belongs to. 0 = zero alloc 1 = 65 .. 96 bytes 2 = 129 .. 192 bytes n = 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}(n-\/1)+1 .. 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}n

Note:\+ \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a9c0e30a0d62f3441f1566ab196bfd94e}{\+\_\+\+\_\+kmalloc\+\_\+index()} is compile-\/time optimized, and not runtime optimized; typical usage is via \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a588ce4944c10e8c2162d0de623c9c2eb}{kmalloc\+\_\+index()} and therefore evaluated at compile-\/time. Callers where !size\+\_\+is\+\_\+constant should only be test modules, where runtime overheads of \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a9c0e30a0d62f3441f1566ab196bfd94e}{\+\_\+\+\_\+kmalloc\+\_\+index()} can be tolerated. Also see \doxylink{mm_2slab_8h_a35c83e79ace64317dc7857607c5d5941}{kmalloc\+\_\+slab()}.

Function Description:\+ Computes the index into the kmalloc cache array for a given size. This is compile-\/time optimized for constant sizes. Returns the index of the cache that can serve the requested size.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\_\_realloc\_size()}{\_\_realloc\_size()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ae06c4998c38440e97132a934b74e642f} 
\+\_\+\+\_\+realloc\+\_\+size (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{2}]{}{, }\item[{3}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

krealloc\+\_\+array -\/ reallocate memory for an array. {\ttfamily }:\+ pointer to the memory chunk to reallocate @new\+\_\+n:\+ new number of elements to alloc @new\+\_\+size:\+ new size of a single member of the array @flags:\+ the type of memory to allocate (see kmalloc)

If \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO logic is requested, callers must ensure that, starting with the initial memory allocation, every subsequent call to this API for the same memory allocation is flagged with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Otherwise, it is possible that \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO is not fully honored by this API.

See \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a543112931ff8a4d330a6de578c0ef6a2}{krealloc\+\_\+noprof()} for further details.

In any case, the contents of the object pointed to are preserved up to the lesser of the new and old sizes.

Function Description:\+ Reallocates memory for an array without allocation profiling. It multiplies new\+\_\+n \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} new\+\_\+size with overflow checking. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a35bd8681126da4d18033311ff65b8365}{krealloc\+\_\+array()}. \Hypertarget{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a5117ca6dc8d9e11ee2bc98623a0a515f}\index{slab.h@{slab.h}!arch\_slab\_minalign@{arch\_slab\_minalign}}

Arches can define this function if they want to decide the minimum slab alignment at runtime. The value returned by the function must be a power of two and \texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}= ARCH\+\_\+\+SLAB\+\_\+\+MINALIGN.

Function Description:\+ Returns the minimum alignment required for slab allocations on the architecture. This can be overridden by architectures to provide runtime alignment requirements. Returns a power of two \texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}= ARCH\+\_\+\+SLAB\+\_\+\+MINALIGN.

kfree -\/ free previously allocated memory @object:\+ pointer returned by \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}, \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a5869820842a9951b09600f4ee9f838d9}{kmalloc\+\_\+nolock()}, or \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a25a85450e1021b8c861f084b722b00d5}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc()}

If @object is NULL, no operation is performed.

Function Description:\+ This is the exported function for freeing memory allocated with \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}, \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_aacbb1d218fb9bc08259e0b2a0ec39701}{kvmalloc()}, or \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a25a85450e1021b8c861f084b722b00d5}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc()}. It handles NULL pointers gracefully. It determines if the object is large (non-\/slab) and calls \doxylink{slub_8c_a5779b41e1a3cd52b95753ddce98e8529}{free\+\_\+large\+\_\+kmalloc()}. Otherwise, it finds the slab and cache and calls \doxylink{slub_8c_a1561d2946d4fe2250acfb3a28b955c84}{slab\+\_\+free()}.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kfree\_nolock()}{kfree\_nolock()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_affd7b1731ba1bd64a6f83006e12692c6} 
void kfree\+\_\+nolock (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{object}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Can be called while holding raw\+\_\+spinlock\+\_\+t or from IRQ and NMI, but ONLY for objects allocated by \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a5869820842a9951b09600f4ee9f838d9}{kmalloc\+\_\+nolock()}. Debug checks (like kmemleak and kfence) were skipped on allocation, hence obj = \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}; kfree\+\_\+nolock(obj); will miss kmemleak/\+kfence book keeping and will cause false positives. large\+\_\+kmalloc is not supported either.

Function Description:\+ This function is a special variant of \doxylink{slub_8c_a97d9c37b943449ab48e32c6d2499261f}{kfree()} for objects allocated with \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a5869820842a9951b09600f4ee9f838d9}{kmalloc\+\_\+nolock()}. It can be called from atomic, interrupt, or NMI contexts. It skips many of the usual debugging checks (like kmemleak) that take locks. It attempts to use the fastpath \doxylink{slub_8c_a8db063671336dc1965b347f60fcb8bdc}{free\+\_\+to\+\_\+pcs()} (without spinning) and if that fails, it defers the free via \doxylink{slub_8c_a8f138aedda49f4d1913e086cabbee81a}{defer\+\_\+free()}. It does not support large kmalloc allocations. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{slub_8c_source_l07221}{7221}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{slub_8c_source}{slub.\+c}}.

void kfree\+\_\+sensitive (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{p}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

kfree\+\_\+sensitive -\/ Clear sensitive information in memory before freeing {\ttfamily }:\+ object to free memory of

The memory of the object {\ttfamily points} to is zeroed before freed. If {\ttfamily is} NULL, \doxylink{slab__common_8c_a2d2646034fc567fc383390023c1fc39c}{kfree\+\_\+sensitive()} does nothing.

Note:\+ this function zeroes the whole allocated buffer which can be a good deal bigger than the requested buffer size passed to \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}. So be careful when using this function in performance sensitive code.

Function Description:\+ Frees memory containing sensitive data. It zeroes the allocated memory before freeing to prevent data leaks. Used for cryptographic or security-\/sensitive data.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmalloc\_nolock\_noprof()}{kmalloc\_nolock\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a1389ea55e4855270120eb72f5ba32d37} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} kmalloc\+\_\+nolock\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfp\+\_\+flags}{, }\item[{int}]{node}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Allocates memory without taking locks. This is used in contexts where locks are already held or not needed. It is a lockless version of \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}.

kmalloc\+\_\+nolock -\/ Allocate an object of given size from any context. @size:\+ size to allocate @gfp\+\_\+flags:\+ GFP flags. Only \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ACCOUNT, \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO, \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+OBJ\+\_\+\+EXT allowed. @node:\+ node number of the target node.

Return:\+ pointer to the new object or NULL in case of error. NULL does not mean EBUSY or EAGAIN. It means ENOMEM. There is no reason to call it again and expect !\+NULL.

Function Description:\+ kmalloc that can be called from NMI/\+atomic contexts. Only supports cmpxchg\+\_\+double architectures. Returns the object or NULL.

\index{kmalloc\_size\_roundup@{kmalloc\_size\_roundup}!slab.h@{slab.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmalloc\_size\_roundup()}{kmalloc\_size\_roundup()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a2b814faa932a75fad705b1b9d5e5d630} 
size\+\_\+t kmalloc\+\_\+size\+\_\+roundup (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

kmalloc\+\_\+size\+\_\+roundup -\/ Report allocation bucket size for the given size

@size:\+ Number of bytes to round up from.

This returns the number of bytes that would be available in a \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()} allocation of @size bytes. For example, a 126 byte request would be rounded up to the next sized kmalloc bucket, 128 bytes. (This is strictly for the general-\/purpose \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}-\/based allocations, and is not for the pre-\/sized \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a25a85450e1021b8c861f084b722b00d5}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc()}-\/based allocations.)

Use this to \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()} the full bucket size ahead of time instead of using \doxylink{slub_8c_aad92a67e3451f0b7971c08ecad26b2e4}{ksize()} to query the size after an allocation.

Function Description:\+ Returns the actual allocation size for a given request size. This rounds up to the next kmalloc bucket size. Used to determine how much memory will actually be allocated.

Function Description:\+ Returns the actual allocation size for a given request. It rounds up to the next kmalloc bucket size or page size for large allocations. Used to determine how much memory will actually be allocated.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmem\_cache\_alloc\_bulk\_noprof()}{kmem\_cache\_alloc\_bulk\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a17a3dbb1756e02b0365ed73ac5dda75b} 
int kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+bulk\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{s}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{flags}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{p}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Allocates multiple objects from a kmem cache in bulk without allocation profiling. This is more efficient than allocating objects individually. Returns the number of objects successfully allocated.

Note that interrupts must be enabled when calling this function and gfp flags must allow spinning.

Function Description:\+ This is the exported function for allocating multiple objects from a cache. It handles the KFENCE allocation (at most one per batch), attempts to get objects from the percpu sheaves using \doxylink{slub_8c_ab731f033d4b70d794faa71b01e93fbb3}{alloc\+\_\+from\+\_\+pcs\+\_\+bulk()}, and fills the rest using \doxylink{slub_8c_a91be793dbf4641469e7732fb02732833}{\+\_\+\+\_\+kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+bulk()}. It then applies post-\/alloc hooks and memory initialization. It returns the number of successfully allocated objects.

Function Description:\+ Allocates an object from a specific sheaf without allocation profiling. This bypasses the normal allocation path to use objects already in the sheaf.

Allocate from a sheaf obtained by \doxylink{slub_8c_ac6fb041f45320469bf732b95eb27ae04}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+prefill\+\_\+sheaf()}

Guaranteed not to fail as many allocations as was the requested size. After the sheaf is emptied, it fails -\/ no fallback to the slab cache itself.

The gfp parameter is meant only to specify \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO or \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ACCOUNT memcg charging is forced over limit if necessary, to avoid failure.

It is possible that the allocation comes from kfence and then the sheaf size is not decreased.

Function Description:\+ Allocates from a pre-\/filled sheaf. Guaranteed not to fail up to the sheaf\textquotesingle{}s size. Returns the object or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Allocates an object from a kmem cache on a specific NUMA node without allocation profiling. This allows controlling which node the memory comes from.

kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+node -\/ Allocate an object on the specified node @s:\+ The cache to allocate from. @gfpflags:\+ See \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}. @node:\+ node number of the target node.

Identical to kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc but it will allocate memory on the given node, which can improve the performance for cpu bound structures.

Fallback to other node is possible if \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+THISNODE is not set.

Return:\+ pointer to the new object or NULL in case of error

Function Description:\+ Allocates an object from a specific NUMA node. Returns the object or NULL.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmem\_cache\_charge()}{kmem\_cache\_charge()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a720e99cf4604474392f2669fef155930} 
bool kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+charge (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{objp}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfpflags}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+charge -\/ memcg charge an already allocated slab memory @objp:\+ address of the slab object to memcg charge @gfpflags:\+ describe the allocation context

kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+charge allows charging a slab object to the current memcg, primarily in cases where charging at allocation time might not be possible because the target memcg is not known (i.\+e. softirq context)

The objp should be pointer returned by the slab allocator functions like kmalloc (with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ACCOUNT in flags) or kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc. The memcg charge behavior can be controlled through gfpflags parameter, which affects how the necessary internal metadata can be allocated. Including \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOFAIL denotes that overcharging is requested instead of failure, but is not applied for the internal metadata allocation.

There are several cases where it will return true even if the charging was not done:\+ More specifically:\+


\begin{DoxyEnumerate}
\item For !\+CONFIG\+\_\+\+MEMCG or cgroup\+\_\+disable=memory systems.
\item Already charged slab objects.
\item For slab objects from KMALLOC\+\_\+\+NORMAL caches -\/ allocated by \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()} without \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ACCOUNT
\item Allocating internal metadata has failed
\end{DoxyEnumerate}

Return:\+ true if charge was successful otherwise false.

Function Description:\+ Charges an already allocated slab object to memcg. Used for late charging when allocation-\/time charging wasn\textquotesingle{}t possible.

Function Description:\+ Destroys a \doxylink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}. It waits for pending RCU frees, decrements the refcount, shuts down the cache, removes it from the list, and releases resources. Returns when the cache is fully destroyed.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmem\_cache\_free()}{kmem\_cache\_free()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a3515b5b594edddb8f4910f5a76b9f4bd} 
void kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+free (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{s}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{x}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+free -\/ Deallocate an object @s:\+ The cache the allocation was from. @x:\+ The previously allocated object.

Free an object which was previously allocated from this cache.

Function Description:\+ This is the exported function for freeing an object from a \doxylink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}. It performs basic validation (if debugging is enabled), records a trace event, and then calls \doxylink{slub_8c_a1561d2946d4fe2250acfb3a28b955c84}{slab\+\_\+free()} to perform the actual freeing. It is the public API for freeing objects allocated with \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a25a85450e1021b8c861f084b722b00d5}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc()}.

\Hypertarget{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ad71879dc5a858fc6743c68d038db566e}\index{slab.h@{slab.h}!kmem\_cache\_free\_bulk@{kmem\_cache\_free\_bulk}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmem\_cache\_free\_bulk()}{kmem\_cache\_free\_bulk()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ad71879dc5a858fc6743c68d038db566e} 
void kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+free\+\_\+bulk (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{s}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{p}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Bulk allocation and freeing operations. These are accelerated in an allocator specific way to avoid taking locks repeatedly or building metadata structures unnecessarily.

Note that interrupts must be enabled when calling these functions.

Function Description:\+ Frees multiple objects back to a kmem cache in bulk. This is more efficient than freeing objects individually. It takes an array of pointers and frees them all.

Note that interrupts must be enabled when calling this function.

Function Description:\+ This is the exported function for bulk freeing objects from a \doxylink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}. If the cache supports sheaves, it calls \doxylink{slub_8c_a5aa401003662200c843ac199bd8e7409}{free\+\_\+to\+\_\+pcs\+\_\+bulk()}. Otherwise, it repeatedly builds detached freelists and calls \doxylink{slub_8c_a9ebc0f21c24b2244a71bd986c50e8f35}{slab\+\_\+free\+\_\+bulk()} to free them.

Function Description:\+ This function is called after the rest of the kernel is initialized to perform any late initialization. It allocates a workqueue for flushing sheaves (slub\+\_\+flushwq) and initializes the random state for freelist randomization. This is the final step in SLUB initialization.

int kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+refill\+\_\+sheaf (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{s}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfp}{, }\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structslab__sheaf}{slab\+\_\+sheaf}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{sheafp}{, }\item[{unsigned int}]{size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Refills a sheaf with more objects from a cache. It adds objects to an existing sheaf up to the specified size. Returns 0 on success or an error code.

refill a sheaf previously returned by kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+prefill\+\_\+sheaf to at least the given size

the sheaf might be replaced by a new one when requesting more than s-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}sheaf\+\_\+capacity objects if such replacement is necessary, but the refill fails (returning -\/ENOMEM), the existing sheaf is left intact

In practice we always refill to full sheaf\textquotesingle{}s capacity.

Function Description:\+ Refills a sheaf to a given size. Handles both regular and oversize sheafs. Returns 0 on success or -\/ENOMEM.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmem\_cache\_return\_sheaf()}{kmem\_cache\_return\_sheaf()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_aca3836ef69ab4612b63b854038baf4a7} 
void kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+return\+\_\+sheaf (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{s}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfp}{, }\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structslab__sheaf}{slab\+\_\+sheaf}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{sheaf}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Returns a sheaf to a cache. This frees the sheaf or returns it to the per-\/node barn for reuse. Called when a sheaf is no longer needed.

Use this to return a sheaf obtained by \doxylink{slub_8c_ac6fb041f45320469bf732b95eb27ae04}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+prefill\+\_\+sheaf()}

If the sheaf cannot simply become the percpu spare sheaf, but there\textquotesingle{}s space for a full sheaf in the barn, we try to refill the sheaf back to the cache\textquotesingle{}s sheaf\+\_\+capacity to avoid handling partially full sheaves.

If the refill fails because gfp is e.\+g. GFP\+\_\+\+NOWAIT, or the barn is full, the sheaf is instead flushed and freed.

Function Description:\+ Returns a sheaf to the cache (spare or barn). Refills the sheaf if possible, otherwise flushes and frees it.

Common kmalloc functions provided by all allocators

Function Description:\+ Reallocates memory with alignment and node affinity without allocation profiling. It changes the size of a previously allocated memory block, preserving the contents up to the smaller size. This is the internal implementation for krealloc with alignment.

krealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align -\/ reallocate memory. The contents will remain unchanged. {\ttfamily }:\+ object to reallocate memory for. @new\+\_\+size:\+ how many bytes of memory are required. @align:\+ desired alignment. @flags:\+ the type of memory to allocate. @nid:\+ NUMA node or NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE

If {\ttfamily is} NULL, \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ac8c9ea33aa7b9f664e1a395d4db27399}{krealloc()} behaves exactly like \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}. If @new\+\_\+size is 0 and {\ttfamily is} not a NULL pointer, the object pointed to is freed.

Only alignments up to those guaranteed by \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()} will be honored. Please see Documentation/\+core-\/api/\+memory-\/allocation.\+rst for more details.

If \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO logic is requested, callers must ensure that, starting with the initial memory allocation, every subsequent call to this API for the same memory allocation is flagged with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Otherwise, it is possible that \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO is not fully honored by this API.

When \doxylink{mm_2slab_8h_a50a3617883868e5066bd41b61e3e568d}{slub\+\_\+debug\+\_\+orig\+\_\+size()} is off, \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ac8c9ea33aa7b9f664e1a395d4db27399}{krealloc()} only knows about the bucket size of an allocation (but not the exact size it was allocated with) and hence implements the following semantics for shrinking and growing buffers with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO:\+:\+ \begin{DoxyVerb}      new             bucket
\end{DoxyVerb}
 0 size size \texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/---\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/-\/---\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|} \texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|} keep \texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|} zero \texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}

Otherwise, the original allocation size \textquotesingle{}orig\+\_\+size\textquotesingle{} could be used to precisely clear the requested size, and the new size will also be stored as the new \textquotesingle{}orig\+\_\+size\textquotesingle{}.

In any case, the contents of the object pointed to are preserved up to the lesser of the new and old sizes.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL in case of error

Function Description:\+ This is the exported function for reallocating memory, with the ability to specify a NUMA node and alignment. It handles the case where new\+\_\+size is 0 by freeing the old pointer and returning ZERO\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+PTR. It calls \+\_\+\+\_\+do\+\_\+krealloc() to perform the reallocation and frees the old pointer if a new one is allocated. This is the no-\/profile version of the function.

It is more efficient to use \doxylink{slub_8c_a97d9c37b943449ab48e32c6d2499261f}{kfree()} or \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} directly if the allocation type is known.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_adde0643cdb4ceba37c4491ed394a9b9c} 
void kvfree\+\_\+rcu\+\_\+barrier (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

kvfree\+\_\+rcu\+\_\+barrier -\/ Wait until all in-\/flight kvfree\+\_\+rcu() complete.

Note that a single argument of kvfree\+\_\+rcu() call has a slow path that triggers synchronize\+\_\+rcu() following by freeing a pointer. It is done before the return from the function. Therefore for any single-\/argument call that will result in a \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a5273884a01705283d2b9e4262a8e6da3}{kfree()} to a cache that is to be destroyed during module exit, it is developer\textquotesingle{}s responsibility to ensure that all such calls have returned before the call to \doxylink{slab__common_8c_a0ed417eb6258e26997a82b132776f54a}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+destroy()}.

Function Description:\+ Waits for all in-\/flight kvfree\+\_\+rcu() calls to complete. It flushes sheaves and calls \doxylink{slab__common_8c_a014e20f10be6906ad5091a5b254444d3}{\+\_\+\+\_\+kvfree\+\_\+rcu\+\_\+barrier()}. Used during module unload and cache destruction.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kvrealloc\_node\_align\_noprof()}{kvrealloc\_node\_align\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ad61d53e7fc764e6d54e97a91fedf59db} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} kvrealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{p}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{align}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{flags}{, }\item[{int}]{nid}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Reallocates memory using kvrealloc with alignment and node affinity without allocation profiling. It handles both kmalloc and vmalloc-\/backed memory. This is the internal implementation for kvrealloc.

kvrealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align -\/ reallocate memory; contents remain unchanged {\ttfamily }:\+ object to reallocate memory for @size:\+ the size to reallocate @align:\+ desired alignment @flags:\+ the flags for the page level allocator @nid:\+ NUMA node id

If {\ttfamily is} NULL, \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ae4cb7883b03514294e90e96af0bff095}{kvrealloc()} behaves exactly like \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_aacbb1d218fb9bc08259e0b2a0ec39701}{kvmalloc()}. If @size is 0 and {\ttfamily is} not a NULL pointer, the object pointed to is freed.

Only alignments up to those guaranteed by \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()} will be honored. Please see Documentation/\+core-\/api/\+memory-\/allocation.\+rst for more details.

If \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO logic is requested, callers must ensure that, starting with the initial memory allocation, every subsequent call to this API for the same memory allocation is flagged with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Otherwise, it is possible that \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO is not fully honored by this API.

In any case, the contents of the object pointed to are preserved up to the lesser of the new and old sizes.

This function must not be called concurrently with itself or \doxylink{slub_8c_ac88c4c777e1cffcd536d365ce527ba94}{kvfree()} for the same memory allocation.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL in case of error

Function Description:\+ This is the exported function for reallocating memory, similar to \doxylink{slub_8c_a4cc2d5d8df87691ba601b518eca3dbdb}{krealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align\+\_\+noprof()}, but it can also handle vmalloc-\/allocated memory. If the existing pointer is from vmalloc, it calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a5e8f69c662b265347214d2aa20e6a490}{vrealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align\+\_\+noprof()}. Otherwise, it calls \doxylink{slub_8c_a4cc2d5d8df87691ba601b518eca3dbdb}{krealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align\+\_\+noprof()}. If the krealloc fails, it falls back to \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a602b13cebb7df2d1fc1da9db991314dd}{kvmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align\+\_\+noprof()} and copies the old data.
